tbmm-2016-07-13sit05spe0373par0120	Mr. President, I have come before you to speak on behalf of the government on the first part of the bill. As I begin with my words, I salute you with respect, with love. The general title of the bill is, you know, the improvement of the investment environment. So, in general, we're trying to eliminate the legal problems that our investors face in today's investment environment; we're particularly trying to eliminate some costs that have arisen during the investment period; especially during the business period, we're trying to eliminate the costs of real indirect processing taxes, such as stamp tax, mortar, bank insurance tax, business taxes; we're trying to make Turkey a real production center, investment center, and employment center. So the bill is really meeting the expectations of the private sector, the market, the NGOs today. When these arrangements come to life, we go to significant reductions in tax burdens on our businesses, especially in the real sector, who want to use money. We significantly reduce the costs our firms face during the investment period, especially when they want to invest, and they want to buy an investment incentive. So there's a bill here that actually contributes to improving the investment climate. Again, we're bringing solutions to the issue of a public bankruptcy postponement, which has been discussed in this bill for some time. True, there have been arrangements in the past to resolve the problems faced in the conditions of the system, whether it is a matter of default or a check. These were well - intentioned arrangements to pave the way for the market at the time, but during practice, we also faced some of the issues that the private sector brought up. The economy is a dynamic process, and the economy can never be a static process. So at a certain time, at a certain time, you make an arrangement to meet the needs that arise in the circumstances of that day, but this time, when actors in the economy behave differently, the problem manifests differently, at a different point. Then what do you have to do? As economic management, as government, you need to solve new issues this time. So in this bill, the main reason behind the arrangements for bankruptcy or the check is the new needs that emerge in the economy after the date of those arrangements, and we all know today that the problems faced in the check must be resolved and must be solved quickly and certainly used again as a payment and loan tool. It's shown here in consultations with both private sector institutions and NGOs that if we don't bring a deterrent sanction in particular about bounced checks, well-intentioned checks can be victims of that. Then what's going on? The check is an obstacle to the widespread use of the market. So we're narrowing down the volume of the loan that we're making on the check in the market by our own hand. So, as a government today, the arrangements that we bring about the check, whether it's about the bankruptcy delay, are the arrangements that have come up with a really large consultation. It was also true when the arrangements were made that day, and today's arrangements are true within the scope of today's needs. Let us certainly consider that this is the dynamic process of economics. As for the bankruptcy delay, of course, the most pressing issue is the bankruptcy delay, which is actually an important institution to keep businesses in the economy running. In modern economies, in modern legal systems, it's a system that sustains companies, protects them in their difficult situations, but in fact protects the rights of real creditors. So in our arrangement, we do not move the institution of bankruptcy to a different point, except for its purpose; on the contrary, we transform it into a structure that is suitable for its purpose. In this framework, we eliminate a number of legal factors that have caused the abuse of the institution, particularly the institution of bankruptcy. In view of such regulations, the right to appeal to creditors is provided to the trade record and the declaration of the request for a postponement of bankruptcy. You know, we don't collect what we're going to receive when the recession process continues to delay due to creditors in bankruptcy. So we're making the system even more active at the point that the creditors who are going to be affected by the bankruptcy delay are aware of the process. We're clarifying the measures that can be submitted on the application for postponement. We include precautionary measures and precautionary foreclosures. Again, it is possible to appeal to the creditors and to apply to other concerns in case they decide to postpone bankruptcy. You know, bankruptcy delays can extend for up to eight years, due to the implementation of current legal regulations. However, the fact that the bankruptcy delay process is taking so long is not an accurate and efficient method for both the owners and the creditors. So we're bringing in new times in the process of postponing bankruptcy. With the arrangement we've made here, we're holding the law on the fact that a one-year recession period could be extended to a maximum of one more year. In addition, in order to attract the liquidation of debt through the concordato, the confirmation office is also authorized by measure to stop measures, stop chases, and ban new pursuit of the concordato confirmation. So I'm hoping that once we've come to terms with this bankruptcy delay, it will become a more efficient, more efficient, and more efficient institution that protects the rights of our companies, but also protects the rights of creditors affected by bankruptcy delays. Again, we're making arrangements in section one for the bank and insurance treatment tax. You know, the tax on bank and insurance is essentially a tax on financial transactions in the financial sector. So here we make an exception to the tax on bank and insurance, which is a cost factor between those who provide financing and those who benefit finance. So we have the opportunity to lower the cost of bank and insurance treatments on real sector companies. Once again, if an insurance agreement has been cancelled regarding the insurance agreements, we are also offering the possibility that the bank and insurance treatment tax, which has been bought accordingly, should not be taxed. Thus, when we consider the arrangements made here in chapter one and in other chapters, we make really important arrangements. With fresh blood that our citizens and other citizens of the country who have brought their assets abroad to Turkey will join the economy, I hope that in the coming period investment will increase, production will increase, employment will increase, the development move that we've made in our country over the last 14 years will continue in line with their 2023 goals. I wish our country, our nation, our investors, the best legal arrangements made in the first chapter of this framework.

"The bankruptcy of companies and co-ops and the postponement of bankruptcy: MADDE 179-If the co-ops and capital companies and co-ops are identified by the court, they will be bankrupt without delay if they are determined by a statement or a court in the event of liquidation by a company or a debtor, according to an interim balance set up over the possible sales prices of the actives. The recovery project shows how all business costs and labour capital will be met during a postponement of objective and real resources and measures, including the introduction of new cash funds. Lists of the current debt payment times and sums, the addresses of creditors, the waiting times and the costs of the operating sector are required to be presented to the court with the latest balance sheet presented to the tax office, the company's or the company's or the management's trade record confirmation and other documents showing that the recovery project is serious and credible based on the continuity of the business. If the list and documents mentioned in the third joke are not submitted with the request for a postponement of bankruptcy, or if not completed by the court within a final two-week period, the request for a postponement of bankruptcy is not proven, and if it is discovered that it is in debt, it is decided to fail the company or the cooperative. A capital company or co-op can't request a postponement of bankruptcy until a year after the end of the postponement period, which has previously taken advantage of the delay of bankruptcy," he said. tbmm-2016-07-13sit07spe0451par0170	Mr. President, honorable lawmakers, the issue of bankruptcy postponement is a matter that has been dealt with as lightly as possible in Turkey. I don't think there's ever been another country in the world where so much capital is despised, pushed around, and really investments are destroyed by people's own hands. Turn around, take a look at the 2008-2009 years. In the United States of America, when the world's largest automotive companies entered into crisis, the United States government took direct action and quickly took the necessary measures to ensure that those companies survive. So let me give you an example. An investor, an international investor who has invested in Libya, is doing very serious work, making serious money, providing employment, and Turkey has a very serious bank loan. That investor wakes up one day in the morning, unable to pay his checks the next day due to a tension in Turkey's foreign policy relations with Libya. Now, what should we do with this investor? Bankrupt? Let him live? By the law you passed, let's bankrupt that investor, just as it happened in hundreds, thousands of tourist facilities in the Mediterranean District after the Russian crisis. What are we going to do with those owners with this legal arrangement? Are we going to bankrupt them, or are we going to keep them in the economy? Friends, all over the world, sectors are made to live on, branding sectors that provide jobs, which provide production. But unfortunately, this legal arrangement of yours allows the industry to be pushed out of the system. Whatever banker sent this, I assure you, this is not an arrangement for the good of this country. Look, let's give you examples of this in the past: When the 2001 crisis took place, the Istanbul Approach was made. Then your government arrived, and in 2006 the Anadolu approach was made. Both were true. Many companies were kept within the system in Turkey with the Istanbul Approach, and many other companies of Turkey were kept within the system in 2006, the same way as the Anatolian Approach. But now, with this arrangement that you brought in, if this had happened in 2001 -- look, I'll give you an example -- in 2001, if the Morning and ATV group, which owed $33 million, had been available with this legal arrangement, it would have gone bankrupt. But then the same group was sold for $1 billion and 150 million, and this country made a lot of money there. Which one was true? In the morning, was it the loss of ATV (ATV) to $33 million, or was it kept within the economic system? Of course, the second method was correct. This is not an operational arrangement. Look, respected lawmakers, let me give you an example: the main reason for the pressure of banks to delay this bankruptcy in particular is that banks are putting their purchases in a very diverse, multitasking pursuit. There is no ban on it, and there is no barrier. The bank puts the bail into a separate contract, a separate follow-up, a follow-up of the Cambio documents he received on it, 3-4 follow-up. He's got 100,000 liras, and he's creating an executive watch for $500,000. $500,000 is taxing the state, embezzling it. Why? Because banks, corporations, have the right to drop the direct tax returns they're going to receive in conflict. Here, you open the door, you allow it. That's wrong. Let me give you another example. He's getting an investment incentive, an investor investing $20 million. That investor's $20 million investment depends on a 100 per cent investment incentive document, the government subsidies him with the tax of the citizen, then he can't pay the $50,000 debt; there's a follow-up, they come, they take out the transformer. 20 million-dollar citizen's investment is being trashed and gone. Don't let them. The world has an economic integrity configuration, a South Korean model. Look, we have an institution in Turkey that has an important fund to consolidate economic integrity, we have an institution called TMSF, they have a very serious savings, they have experience. If you sell economic integrity, if you keep your economy alive, if you prevent bankruptcy, you keep the job alive, people don't get unemployed, banks have the hope of collecting what they're going to get, but if you squander economic integrity like expiration, execution surveillance and so on, the economies won't survive. This bankruptcy postponement should be an institution that needs to be reconsidered. This method is wrong, unsustainable. I salute you all with respect and love.

While the institution for postponing bankruptcy has previously existed as a concept in our law, it has been heavily implemented in 2003 by the amendment to the Law on Emancipation and Bankruption. The postponing of bankruptcy is based on a delicate balance between debtors' interests and even national and regional interests. This can trigger the transfer of debts and the rise of new bankruptcy by dominoes.
